# TailorIT Autofill: architecture

The decision record for the extension, from the plan the owner approved on 2026-10-29. The owner's specification is [docs/SPEC.md](SPEC.md) and is binding. The detailed tables (rules, patterns, golden cases, DDL, routes) are in [docs/design/](design/); where a design report and this file differ, this file wins.

## Context
**The request (2026-10-29).** Build an autofill extension like Jobright Autofill and Simplify Copilot.
- One click fills Greenhouse, Lever, Ashby, Workday and Workable applications from a profile and a resume fetched from TailorIT's server (Workable since v3 Phase 7a). Since v3 Phases 6 and 7b it knows the public pages of Workday, iCIMS, Taleo, SuccessFactors and Oracle Recruiting Cloud (their postings, their entry, their sign-in and account pages); since the owner's snapshots of 2026-11-06 it fills Workday's, iCIMS's and Oracle's application forms by their own rules (Taleo's and SuccessFactors' get the standard rules), and SmartRecruiters and fifteen more ATSs (Jobvite, JazzHR, BambooHR, Rippling, Breezy HR, CareerPlug, Pinpoint, Paycor, HiBob, UltiPro, Dayforce, Paylocity, Dice, ClearCompany and ADP Workforce Now) by theirs.
- What cannot be filled confidently is listed for review (nothing is drawn over the page's fields: docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md §11), and the user submits.
- The owner's specification ([SPEC.md](SPEC.md), verbatim) is binding.

**Owner decisions (2026-10-29, asked during planning):**
- **Repo:** the extension lives in a separate folder and git repository, `F:\Develop\TailorIT-autofill`, and talks to TailorIT only over HTTP. Shared types are copied, not imported.
- **Branch:** TailorIT's own changes (pairing, profile, resume, AI endpoints, the settings pages) go on a new branch from v1.1.
- **LLM:** LLM steps run through TailorIT's Claude CLI queue, and code validates every output (the CLI has no temperature flag).
- **Hidden inputs:** a visually hidden native radio or checkbox is fillable when a visible, non-camouflaged label is tied to it (`label for=`, a wrapping label, or an adapter-declared widget), like the file-input rule. Otherwise it is a honeypot.
- **Review panel:** it comes in both forms, the browser's side panel and an in-page panel (like Simplify's), with a setting to choose. Both mount the same report components.
- **Profile:** the autofill profile is edited in TailorIT (a new Settings › Autofill page, seeded from the default resume). The extension only reads it (until v3 Phase 3b: the side panel's "Your autofill information" saves it too, through TailorIT's own checks).

## Interpretations awaiting your word (each stays off until you turn it on)
These go beyond a literal reading of the specification. Until you approve one, the field it concerns is left blank and flagged.
1. **A legal question that names no country** ("Are you legally authorized to work?"). It would be answered from the profile's entry for the posting's country, which the adapter reads from the page, and flagged.
2. **Several explicit races on a single-choice race question.** It would be answered "Two or More Races", the EEO-1 definition, and flagged. EEO-1 also lets an explicit Hispanic or Latino answer override race on such a question; that stays off too unless you say otherwise.

Since the owner's amendments (2026-10-31/11-01, `docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md`) both are on by default: interpretation 1 reads the posting's country from TailorIT's lookup (its job location), else the profile's only work-authorization entry; and a third, the decline default for unanswered self-identification questions, joins them.

## The owner's amendments: fill everything (2026-10-31/11-01)
`docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md` records them; `docs/SPEC.md` stays verbatim. In the code:
- **Settings version 2** (`src/core/types/settings.ts`): `llm.answers` and `llm.drafts` on, `llm.confirmDrafts` off (gone since version 6, v4), `llm.whenForbidden` `profile-only`; the usual consents pre-approved (privacy, terms, data retention, certify, background check, signature; never marketing or SMS); the three interpretations on. A stored version 1 value is migrated by the worker (`background/settings.ts`), each choice still on its old default taking the new one.
- **AI answers** (`core/plan/answers.ts`, `background/ai-tasks.ts`, TailorIT's `POST /api/autofill/ai/answer`): a question nothing else settled (an unknown question once classification knows no category, a short free-text box, a job preference the profile lacks, a list only the AI classified whose match tied) is asked with its visible text and options only, behind the same screens as drafts (the classify screen, E2's context). A choice comes back as an `llm-pick` of one of the field's own options (its label's hash checked again by the frame), a text as the new provenance `llm-answer {taskId, basis}`. Both are written verified with `needsUserAction: false`: reason `llm-answer-written`, or `ai-guess` when the basis is `guess`. The Guard lets `llm-answer` fill only unknown and free-text questions and `ANSWERABLE_PROFILE_CATEGORIES`.
- **Drafts accepted:** `planDraft` rows a draft `llm-draft-written` (verified) unless `llm.confirmDrafts` (until v4; since settings version 6 always); the final Guard reads an accepted draft as confirmed, and the document books it so a later run rows it the same. A free-text question only the AI classified gets a draft.
- **Profile only** (`content/orchestrator.ts`): a posting that forbids AI follows `llm.whenForbidden`. `profile-only` skips the policy card, marks every `plan/request` `noAi` (the planner then asks the LLM nothing: `PlanInput.noAi`), and shows `policy-profile-only`; `ask` is the Phase 2 card; `use-ai` shows the `policy` notice and fills on. (Since v4 there is no card and no `ask`, and the side panel draws no notice: "v4" below.) A plan answer's `aiAttestation` (an "I did not use AI" box in the frame's scan) switches the run the same way (`ai-attestation`), unless `use-ai`.
- **Never guessed:** EEO takes `eeo.decline-default` (the form's decline option) when the profile has no answer or the sensitive part is not shared, blank when it failed to load; criminal history reads the sensitive part's `legal.criminalRecord` by polarity; consent, attention checks and the AI attestation as before (since 2026-11-01: the next section).
- **Deterministic answers:** `preferences.travel` from `travelPercent`; `screening.previouslyEmployed` from the profile's employers and the lookup's company (`screening.previously-employed`).
- **Edit with AI:** the plan answer's `aiEditable` names the text boxes the page may offer it on (`drafts.ts aiEditableField`); the content's `.ai-edits` layer (`content/ui/ai-edits.ts`) draws one `button[aria-label="Edit with AI"]` in each while the run rests (not in a dry run or a profile-only run), watched by IntersectionObserver v2 like the launcher. A trusted click on a visible button sends `draft/rewrite` (the frame's scan, the field, the canaries, the posting text), the worker plans the field as a rewrite (`PlanInput.rewrite`, the draft's `currentAnswer`), and `plan/poll` brings the draft, written through `writeField` and both Guards. One rewrite at a time.
- **The Phase E review's fixes** (what follows holds over the lines above where they differ):
  - *Consent:* data retention is the employer retaining or keeping the application on file; being contacted, emailed or told about future roles, job alerts and a talent community or pool are marketing (`labelRules.ts` refinements), never pre-approved by default. A radio or a list that asks for such a consent with no consent verb is never given an AI answer (`namesConsentTopic`).
  - *The AI attestation:* only a real "I did not use AI" statement (`attestsNoAiUse`: label.consent-ai's wording or its negation pattern) switches a frame to profile-only; consent that merely names AI is still never ticked.
  - *Context (E2):* a box's section's other questions close it only when every one of them is closed (a self-identification or legal block); in a section that also holds open questions (Greenhouse's one section) only the question it follows up and the one that revealed it do.
  - *AI answers never state* another person's details, an identifier, a postal address or a security clearance (`namesUnanswerableTopic`, `namesAnotherPerson`), screened again by the worker. A lazy list an answer would fill is rowed `waiting-for-options`, so the loop reads it and asks with its options. A plan poll that brings a key planned again and asked anew counts as progress, so each task of a classify-then-answer chain has its own wait cap.
  - *Criminal history:* the person's answer is to "Have you ever been convicted of a crime?": a generic conviction question takes it; a narrower one (a felony, a misdemeanour, a time, unspent, exclusions) only a No; an arrest, a charge or a registration never (`criminal-not-covered`, flagged).
  - *"Have you worked here before?":* the rule's value only for a question that names the posting's company or points at it ("here", "this company", "us", "former employee"); any other employer's question goes to the AI, marked. A travel text box takes the profile's travel share. A posting location naming more than one country (with its regions') names none.
  - *Edit with AI:* offered and asked only while `llm.drafts` is on; never a box whose text names a sensitive answer, a sum, a contact detail or a postal address (`namesSensitiveAnswer`, by the core and again by the worker's `draftBatches`). The first `noAi` request marks the run in the worker's memory: its later plans and polls ask no AI and `draft/rewrite` is refused; the content's loop gives up what still waits (`ai-off-for-posting`) and writes no AI value that came in (`LoopEnv.aiOff`).
  - *The settings migration:* a version 1 install with the tiebreak and classification both off keeps answers off and drafts as they were.


## The owner's decisions of 2026-11-01: determine all by AI (docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md §11)
What follows holds over the section above where they differ.
- **Settings version 3** (`src/core/types/settings.ts`): `llm.answerAll` ("Let the AI answer legal, pay and attention-check questions your profile doesn't answer", on by default; it acts only while `llm.answers` is on, and the Options page disables it then). A stored version 2 value keeps every choice and takes it at its default (`migrateSettingsV2`); a version 1 value goes through version 2's migration first.
- **Which questions** (`core/plan/answers.ts` `answerScope`): a field is asked in the standard scope as before, else, with `answerAll`, in TailorIT's `all` scope (`AnswerItem.scope`) when it is an attention check with no consent of its own (`classification.by === 'attention'`), a category of `AI_OPENED_CATEGORIES` (the legal categories, `salary.expected`, `salary.currency`, `attentionCheck`) whose row says the profile holds no answer (`legal-not-in-profile`, `criminal-not-covered`, `no-profile-value`, or `work-auth-country-unknown` while the profile holds no work-authorization entry; never `sensitive-not-shared`, where the person may hold an answer this browser may not read), a question the classify screen kept from the LLM, or a gap the standard scope left only for its context. Its own text must name only open topics (`classify.ts` `sensitiveTopicsOf`, `openAnswerTopics`: the screen's terms grouped by topic, `OPEN_SCREEN_TOPICS` authorization, age, pay, criminal; a consent only in an attention check's own words), never an EEO answer among its options, a decline option only beside an open topic, no birth date; a clearance counts as work authorization. Its context (`drafts.ts` `inClosedContext(…, true)`, `closesOpenAnswer`) may hold legal, pay and attention-check questions but no self-identification, consent or pronoun question. A rule's hand-off to the person still lets it through for what an answer can say (`OPENABLE_CAUSES`: a legal question on a text box, one read from its help text, authorization tied to sponsorship, an unreadable time, a work permit, a nationality, an age band on a choice), never current pay or a source conflict. `namesUnanswerableTopic` lets a clearance through in that scope, and a bare "number" in a pay question or an attention check; another person, an identifier, a postal address, a consent topic, an "I did not use AI" statement (`attestsNoAiUse`) and a question whether AI wrote the application (`AI_USE_QUESTION`) are never sent in any scope.
- **The worker** (`background/ai-tasks.ts`): `answerBatches` sends each scope's items in batches of their own (`AutofillAnswerRequest.scope`), so ordinary questions stay in the standard scope; an `all` item is screened again by `openItemWithheld` (`openTopicsOfTexts` with the vocabulary, `allowanceOf`). Only the question's visible text, hint, section and options travel; the facts are TailorIT's own reading, never a sensitive value.
- **The Guard** (`guard/provenance.ts` `checkCategoryPolicy`): with `GuardContext.answerAll` (the setting on and the AI allowed for the frame) an `llm-pick` or `llm-answer` may fill a category of `AI_OPENED_CATEGORIES`; nothing else changes for the closed classes.
- **Consent** (`resolver/policy.ts` `consentValue`, `ConsentContext`): a required box (or Yes/I agree radio) is ticked by `consent.required` whatever its kinds, binding clauses and unrecognised consent included, a required signature typing the full name; an optional one by `consent.preapproved` only when every kind it names is pre-approved, else it is skipped (`consent-optional-unticked`, never needs-you). An AI attestation (any consent naming AI) is ticked by `consent.no-ai-attestation` only when the run used no AI at all (`ResolveContext.aiFree`: a profile-only frame, and `PlanInput.aiUsed` unset: the worker's `RunAi.used()`, the application's noted tasks `AiTaskIds.any()`, a cover letter its AI wrote), else it is left for the person (`ai-attestation-ai-used`; since v4, D1, left unticked and listed as not filled, never the person's). `USER_ONLY_CATEGORIES` keeps its members out of the profile, saved answers and LLM classification; each has its one way in (`OWN_RULES` in the Guard).
- **A consent asked as a choice** (`resolver/classifyRules.ts` `consentChoice`, `consentChoiceOption`; `labelRules.ts` `consentChoiceHit`, `affirmativeOption`; the live BeyondTrust form's "Please review and acknowledge BeyondTrust's" with its one option "Acknowledge/Confirm", 2026-11-01): `classifyField(spec, vocab)` reads a list, combobox or radio group the rules leave open (no rule, a consent handed over only for its kind, or a sensitive-topic hand-off that is only consent wording) as consent when its one choosable option (a placeholder aside) plainly agrees and its label with that option is consent wording; its kinds are `consentEntries`' (an object no kind names is `consent.other`, AI named is an AI attestation; a signature is never chosen). `consentValue` (`ConsentContext.choice`) chooses that option by its own label where it would tick a box, under every consent rule above; a combobox whose options are not read yet waits for them (`waiting-for-options`, required or not), so it is decided on what the list offers. Several choosable options, one that does not agree, one disabled, a list read only in part or any option with injected text cut: no consent, the field read as before. `CATEGORY_KINDS` is unchanged (the reading is options-driven, outside the label rules). `assertNotSubmit` no longer reads "confirm" as a submit word in an option of an open list (`role="option"` in a `role="listbox"`, no button, link or input itself and holding none): "Acknowledge/Confirm" chooses a value, while "I confirm and submit" is still refused; the e2e spies read the words the same way.
- **The basis of the right to work** (`resolver/workBasis.ts` `basisClassOf`, `readBasisOptions`; `policy.ts` `basisOfEntry`, `workBasisValue`, `basisPickContradicts`): a list of the questions `BASIS_CATEGORIES` names (authorization, sponsorship at any time, a status not read for the posting's country) whose options read as a basis set (each one exactly one of permanent, a permit needing no sponsorship, sponsorship by its words' meaning, with a permanent and a sponsorship option, none injected) takes the option the entry states: sponsorship needed now or later, else (authorized) a citizen or permanent resident the permanent one, a visa or other status with both sponsorship answers a no the permit one, by `legal.work-basis` (`legal.posting-country` under interpretation 1, flagged), a declined deciding answer the form's decline option. An entry that does not settle it is `legal-not-in-profile`, which the `all` scope asks with every option; `planAnswer` refuses an AI pick the entry's explicit answers deny (`llm-pick-rejected`). A basis the form has no option for is `no-match`.
- **A field made required by the run's own answers** (`core/loop/bookkeeping.ts` `madeRequired`, pending cause `required`; BeyondTrust's demographic consent box, required once a demographic answer is chosen): a field whose row was planned optional and wrote nothing, nothing of ours in it, found required at the scan after a pass that wrote, is planned again; its new row records it as required, so the page's toggling plans it no more. A dry run writes nothing, so it never applies.
- **No highlights** (`content/ui`): `highlights.ts`, the `.hl` rules, `COPY.chips`, `COPY.draftChip`, `COPY.removeSelection`, the final Guard's `removeSelection`, the step machine's `highlight-next` and `highlight-submit` effects and `palette.ts`'s `ACCENT` are gone. `field/focus` (Show on page) scrolls the field into view and focuses its control (`content/targets.ts` `showField`). The visibility watch (`visible.ts`) and the trusted-click checks of the launcher, the card, the panel and Edit with AI are unchanged.

## Cover letters (v2 Phase G, 2026-11-01)
A form's cover-letter upload (`file.coverLetter`) gets the person's letter for the job from TailorIT (Documents › Cover Letters), written by TailorIT's cover-letter AI when they have none and AI may write it. TailorIT's routes (API version 2, scope `letters:write`): `POST /api/autofill/cover-letters {jobId?, company, jobTitle, jobText?, resumeId, generate, canaries?}` → `{letterId, state: ready | generating | failed, aiWritten}` (`aiWritten`: TailorIT's AI is writing it, or wrote it and the person never edited it; TailorIT v1.2 `ac15c30`), `GET …/:id` → `{state}`, `GET …/:id/file` → the PDF (`X-TailorIT-File-Kind: pdf`, `X-TailorIT-Sha256`, a Content-Disposition name; 409 until ready, 413 over 10 MiB).
- **The plan** (`core/plan/frame.ts` `planCoverLetter`): a writable field the rules read as `file.coverLetter`, empty and with nothing but the letter missing, waits for the run's letter (`PlanOutput.coverLetter {keys, generate}`: never in `awaiting`, which holds LLM questions only; the planner adds the keys to the answer's `awaiting`, so the frame polls them like the AI's). `generate` is whether TailorIT's AI may write it: AI-written answers on (`llm.drafts`), not a profile-only frame (the posting forbids AI, or an "I did not use AI" attestation), never a dry run. Its row while it waits is `cover-letter-waiting` (flagged).
- **Asking** (`background/cover-letters.ts`, once per run, by the first plan that has one to fill): the lookup's job (its id, company and title, held in the worker's memory only), else the names the frame's page title gives (`plan/request`'s `posting`: Greenhouse's "Job Application for <title> at <company>", Lever's "<company> - <title>"; a title that holds a tripwire names nothing); the resume the run attaches (`resume-choice.ts`); and, only with `generate`, the posting's visible text as the frame read it (`plan/request`'s `postingText`: '' for a form embedded in another page, never TailorIT's stored copy) and the page's canaries. With `generate` false TailorIT answers only a letter the person wrote or edited (404 when there is none). It asks in two steps (the Phase G review): first a check (`generate` false, no page text, no canaries: TailorIT's 60 checks a minute, apart from its writes), then, only with `generate` and when the check found none to use (a 404, or `failed`: an empty letter TailorIT writes into, or the person's unfinished one), the ask that may write (10 a minute and 60 an hour per account). `generating` is polled every `LETTER_POLL_MS` (2 s: 30 a minute against TailorIT's 120 state reads a minute; a 429's hint when longer) for at most `LETTER_CAP_MS` (180 s, then `cover-letter-timed-out`). A 409 `ai_busy` is asked again after `LETTER_BUSY_MS` (15 s, 30 s, then 60 s: at most 5 asks that may write within the cap), a 429 after its hint; a hint past the cap ends the ask at once as TailorIT's limit (`cover-letter-unavailable`, `limited`; the side panel's `rate-limited`), not a time-out. Nothing is asked without a resume, nor for a posting TailorIT lacks whose page names no company or title (`cover-letter-unavailable`), nor for an input whose accept list refuses a PDF (`format-unsupported`, in the plan: `core/accept.ts` `accepts`).
- **Settled:** `ready` is planned again as the profile's document (`withCoverLetter`: `profile.coverLetter {aiWritten}`, only once ready) through `guardWrite`: `{kind: 'file', ref: 'cover-letter'}`, provenance `profile`, path `coverLetter`; its row `cover-letter-attached` (the person's letter), or `cover-letter-written` when TailorIT's AI wrote it: TailorIT's `aiWritten` (a letter the AI wrote before the run, from Write it now or the letter editor's Generate, that the person never edited, as much as one written now), or a `generating` answer for this ask (the run's value-free record keeps `letterWritten`, so a worker that restarted still knows), which the review marks "AI" (`aiMarkOf`). An AI-written letter is never attached once the frame is profile-only (`ai-off-for-posting`) or with AI-written answers off (`drafts-disabled`); the person's own still is. `failed` is `cover-letter-failed` (TailorIT could not write it, or the person's letter still has a placeholder), a 404 to `generate` false `cover-letter-none`, a 403 `cover-letter-reconnect` (a connection made before `letters:write`), any other failure `cover-letter-unavailable`. A field that already holds a file is `prefill-kept`, never asked for.
- **The Guard** (provenance table, `core/guard/provenance.ts`): an upload takes only its own category's document: `file.resume` the profile path `resume`, `file.coverLetter` the path `coverLetter`, `file.other` nothing; and in the frame too, a file write's origin must name the document its value carries (`FILE_PATHS[ref]`, no transform). This narrows what an upload took before (any upload took `profile: resume`); nothing is weakened.
- **The file:** the frame's write opens the resume port with `resume/request {runId, document: 'cover-letter'}`; the worker takes the run's ready letter id from its memory (none: `resume-unavailable`, before any fetch; a worker that holds no letter for the run, having restarted since the plan or changed token, checks TailorIT again for the person's own, `checkLetter`: `generate` false, the lookup read again, so nothing is written and no AI letter is answered), fetches the PDF (at most `AUTOFILL_COVER_LETTER_MAX_BYTES`, 10 MiB), accepts it only as a PDF (`X-TailorIT-File-Kind: pdf`, `%PDF-`) whose SHA-256 is `X-TailorIT-Sha256`, names it as TailorIT's Download does (Content-Disposition, cleaned by `uploadName`, else "Cover Letter.pdf"), and sends it in the resume's chunks, acknowledgements, hash check and 60 s release. The frame maps a failed transfer to `cover-letter-unavailable`, an input that takes no PDF to `format-unsupported`.
- **The frame's wait** (`content/loop.ts`): a letter key is polled like the AI's, under `LETTER_WAIT_CAP_MS` (185 s) while one waits, and given up as `cover-letter-timed-out` (`cover-letter-unavailable` when the worker cannot answer). A run that turns profile-only keeps waiting for it, and drops one that arrives marked `cover-letter-written`.
- **A dry run** asks TailorIT only whether the person has a letter (`generate` false: no page text, no canaries) and writes nothing; a none where a fill would have TailorIT's AI write one is `cover-letter-would-write` (verified, provenance `profile`, as the would-be write's row). The resume port refuses a dry run's letter.
- **The side panel's row** (`panel/letter {tabId, write}`, `ui/panel/{letter.ts, JobCard.tsx}`): under the resume on the job card, "Your letter for this job" when TailorIT has one (`write` false: the card's job and resume, nothing of the page unless TailorIT lacks the job, whose names the frame's `job/postingText` gives), else "Written by AI when the form asks for one", with **Write it now** (`write` true: the frame's visible posting text and canaries, through `job/postingText`): Writing…, then Ready or Couldn't write it. Explained, with no button, with AI-written answers off and where the tab fills from the profile only: a posting that forbids AI under the default setting, or a run of the tab that went profile-only (`fillsFromProfileOnly`: `policy-profile-only`, `ai-attestation`); the worker refuses `ai-off` too, and so for a form whose fields state "I did not use AI" (`job/postingText`'s `attestation`, read as planFrame reads it: `core/plan/attestation.ts`) unless the setting is "Use AI anyway". A dry run of the tab (its latest report) offers no Write it now, and the worker refuses one (`unavailable`), as it does when the form frame's page cannot be read (`job/postingText` unanswered). "Ask me each time" leaves Write it now to the person's click, and "Use AI anyway" writes. A fill's own row of the letter says more than an earlier answer. States only (`LETTER_OUTCOMES`): never an id or a word of a letter. Kept per document, resume and connection for `JOB_CARD_TTL_MS`; six a minute per tab. (Since v4 the card shows no such row and no Write it now; `write: false` is still asked, to tell whether the review's cover-letter row has a letter to preview: "v4" below.)
- **Never:** a letter's id or job id in a message to a frame or the panel, a log line or storage; a word of a letter anywhere but the file the input holds; a letter written by TailorIT's AI in a dry run or a profile-only run.

One question for the Phase 0 stop: legacy `boards.greenhouse.io` pages and embeds now redirect (301) to `job-boards.greenhouse.io` (checked 2026-10-29). Unless you can still capture a live legacy form, Phase 2 takes job-boards.greenhouse.io's native controls and its embed, and its custom widgets move to Phase 4.

## v3 Phase 3a: the panel in Jobright's layout, the modes, the launcher and the lookup on load (docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md §12, Decisions 3 to 5)
What follows holds over every note below where they differ. The plan: `wise-percolating-kite.md`, Phase 3 (its extension-only part; the profile editor, Edit with AI's instruction, "Anything else", the resume preview, the typed cover letter, "Ask TailorIT to support this site" and "Autofill for another job" come with Phase 3b and TailorIT).
- **The progress** (`src/shared/report.ts` `progressOf`, `rowProgressOf`, `isReady`; `ui/report/model.ts` `progressStateOf`, `fieldListsOf`): one rule for the side panel, the in-page panel and the launcher's badge. A row is `filled` (ready: verified, filled, flagged with its value written, or a question that held its answer or needed none), `missing` (it needs the person: `needsPerson`; since v4 `notFilled`, a field the run did not fill, `needsPerson` being a hard-rule stop alone), `working` (its frame's run writes it now or will this pass: `FrameReport.filling`) or `none` (left alone, needing nothing: left out). The percent is the required rows filled of the required rows counted, or of every counted row when none is required, rounded down. The state: `scanning` (a run works and nothing is counted), `filling`, `rest`, `empty`.
- **Live reports** (`content/orchestrator.ts` `liveProgress`, `scheduleLive`, `liveReport`; `content/loop.ts` `LoopEnv.progress`): the loop tells its run the rows it knows (DOM order), the keys still to write this pass and the one written now, before each write and after the pass; the run keeps the latest and sends a `report/live` at most every `LIVE_REPORT_MS` (400 ms) with `filling {current, pending}`, built when its own limiter's slot comes (the worker gives `report/live` a budget of its own, so it never holds back a `report/put`), never renewing the run, never awaited by the loop, and dropped once the step's fill has ended (a gate that moves then, so its own report, which the run awaits, has the last word). A scan's start sends one with no rows and an empty `filling` ("Scanning the page"). The worker stores and shows a live report like any, but it feeds neither the anonymous counters nor the accepted-match cache, and a report after it is counted against the run's own bookkeeping alone. The launcher's badge shows the percent while it fills (`LauncherView.percent`).
- **Rows flip live** (`orchestrator.ts` `edit`, at rest; `shared/report.ts` `filledByPerson`, `leftForPerson`): the person's trusted edit of a field the run left for them sets the row's `detail.filledByYou` while the field holds an answer (and drops it when it is cleared), a flag read from the field and never its value; the lists then show it filled and the counts (the review's "left for you", the launcher's badge) no longer count it. Its card stays open, so Save my answer is still offered where it may be (until v4: since D6 it is offered nowhere).
- **Skip** (`content/loop.ts` `CurrentWrite`, `writeSkippable`; `orchestrator.ts` `skipField`; `runtime.ts` `run/skip`; `background/panel.ts` `skip`; `ui/report/Report.tsx` `SkipButton`): each write sees a signal its Skip aborts and a `stopped()` its Skip turns on (an `Object.create` over the run's env, so the form root and the canaries are still read at each use). Skip after `SKIP_AFTER_MS` (2 s) on the row being written: `panel/skip {tabId, frameId, fieldKey}` → `run/skip {fieldKey}` to that frame alone → the run gives up that write if it is the one being made (`expired` otherwise). A write that was made stands; one given up is `skipped-by-you` (flagged), and its field is filtered out of every later plan and queue of the run. A row the Guard blocked keeps its block. The side panel's clock is animation frames (`ui/page/frames.ts` `afterFrames`), never a timer.
- **The report's look** (`ui/report/Report.tsx`, `styles.ts`): the progress line (a toggle that folds the lists; Minimized folds them at first) with the percent and Cancel while a run works, a bar (indeterminate while scanning), "Reading the form" while scanning; a frame's line only when it says more (a pause and why, a decision, a sign-in, a stop, a step, a dry run, hidden fields; with several forms or an embedded one, each named); the repeatable sections ("Education · 2 of 3 added", `FrameReport.repeatables` from the run's Add outcomes); "Required" then "Optional" (`section.tia-group[data-group]`), each row `li[data-field-key][data-status][data-provenance][data-draft][data-layout][data-progress][data-current]` with its state icon, its label as Show on page, its marks and a chevron (`button[aria-expanded]`) over the detail: the status chip and reason, the facts, the actions. A row that needs the person is unfolded at first. Under the lists: what is left for the person, the AI's answers, "Nothing needs you". (Since v4 a row is a tick or an empty circle and its label, with none of the rest: "v4" below.)
- **The side panel** (`ui/panel/Panel.tsx`): the brand and the gear's menu (`button[data-panel-menu]`: the modes through `ui/page/modes.tsx`, saved at once, and "All settings", `a[data-open-settings]` to `options.html#settings-modes`); problem strips only; the launcher hidden on this site or everywhere (`panel/launcher`) with "Show the TailorIT button again" (`panel/showLauncher`, which clears both flags and sends the tab's frames `launcher/prefs`); a page Autofill does not work on (`page.support` 'none', `section[data-unsupported]` with the sites); the notices; the job card (`section[data-job-card]`: the job's card, then Autofill with Dry run, Continue and Cancel on a tinted strip under it (only Autofill while a run works), then what the fill uses, `div[data-job-docs]`, folded while a run works: "Your autofill information", the resume, and the cover letter only when the form asks for one, `PageState.letter` from the frame's hello or a report's cover-letter row); the progress and the lists; the custom question; at the foot "Connected as …" and the jobs link. The match ring shows its band (`MATCH_BANDS`: Excellent 80, Good 60, Fair 40, else Poor) and a tip (`[role="tooltip"][data-match-tip]`); an "Applied" badge (`[data-job-applied]`) from the tracker's Applied (code 2) on. (Since v4 the panel has views: the gear opens the Settings view, not a menu; there is no Dry run, Continue or Cancel button, no "what the fill uses" box, no notices and no foot, the account showing in Settings: "v4" below.)
- **The modes** (`core/types/settings.ts` version 4, `shared/settings.ts` `ModesSchema`): `modes {signIn, next, afterPageTurn, submit, consents, ai, agentSkip}` and `view`; `migrateSettingsV3` (version 3's `autoAdvance` → `modes.next`, `llm.answerAll` → `modes.ai`), and versions 1 and 2 through it. Content scripts get `modes` with the rest of their part (`ContentSettings`). Wired now: Next (the orchestrator's auto-advance), Autofill after page turn (`orchestrator.ts` `drive`: a `TRANSITIONED` to a new step under Manually goes to `pageTurned`: the rows cleared, the `page-turned` notice, the run offers Continue; `machine.ts` `RunContext.fillAfterPageTurn` false makes a reload offer Continue too), Consents (`core/resolver/policy.ts` `consentValue`: under 'all' every kind the extension recognises is approved; an AI attestation and an unrecognised consent keep their own rules) and AI answers (`core/plan/frame.ts`: the `all` scope while `modes.ai` is 'all'). Sign-in, Submit and the agent's skip rules are stored and not shown.
- **The launcher at the edge** (`content/ui/edge.ts`, `page-ui.ts`, `background/launcher.ts`): the top frame's launcher sits in a tab fixed at the window's right edge (top 120 px by default), with a close button above it (the hide menu: until next visit, on this site, everywhere) and a grip under it (a trusted pointerdown on the visible grip, then its captured trusted moves, y only, kept within the window; ArrowUp and ArrowDown too). The drop is `launcher/place {top}` (a fraction of the window's height); a hide is `launcher/hide {scope}`; "until next visit" is the document's memory. The worker keeps them in `storage.local` `launcher:v1` by a hash of the host (`siteKeyOf`, at most 200 sites) and answers them in `frame/hello`'s answer (`launcher {top, hidden}`). The tab hides while the person hid it, while the in-page panel is open, and while the browser's side panel shows the tab: the side panel's `panel/watch` carries `side: true`, and the worker sends the tab's top frame `panel/side {open}` when the first side panel shows it and when the last one leaves (`reports.ts` `sideMoved`). Its close button, grip and menu items follow the visible-click rule (`page-ui.ts` `act`: a drag's start at once, a menu choice after the watch's first word). The notices and the policy card (since v4 the toasts, fixed at the viewport's top-right in the top frame) stay in their column at the form's corner, but never closer to the shown tab than `EDGE_CLEARANCE_PX` (24 px): IntersectionObserver v2 counts a box-shadow's ink as painted over what lies under it, and a card's shadow reaches about 18 px, so a column any closer hides the launcher and the grip from the visibility watch and their clicks are refused (e2e 60 found it). A frame inside another page keeps its launcher at the form's corner (S1).
- **The lookup on load** (`background/index.ts` `frame/hello`): a hello from a frame with a form, while connected, calls the card's own read (`jobs.card`), so the frame relays `job/lookup` and the card waits in memory for the panel; the worker's tests turn it off (`WorkerOptions.lookupOnLoad`) except where they test it.

The Phase 3a review (it holds over the notes above where they differ):
- **Skip after the page was touched** (`content/pass.ts` `PassEnv.runSignal`, `afterWait`; `dom/widgets/interact.ts` `skipTidyContext`, `closingContext`): a Skip that ends a wait before the write touched the page throws `RunAborted` as before (skipped-by-you). Once the page was touched (a file put into its input, an option clicked, a result chosen), Skip only ends the wait: the write's result stands, is read back after the settle and seen by the final Guard (an upload not confirmed yet is `upload-not-confirmed`). What a widget write opened or typed and did not keep is put back on a fresh signal capped at `SKIP_TIDY_CAP_MS` (1.5 s) that only the run's own end aborts: a typeahead's typed text restored and its list closed, a combobox's list our click opened closed. The worker's match of a typeahead's results (`widgets.plan`) is raced with the Skip. A skipped field is never planned or written again on its step, in a later attempt too (`LoopCarry.skipped`). Skip also reaches the wait for the AI's answers: while the loop waits on them alone, the first waiting field is the current one (`LoopEnv.progress`'s `current`), and its Skip gives up that field's answer (`skipAwaiting`: skipped-by-you, never planned again). A lazy list's read (its options, capped at `OPTIONS_CAP_MS`, 5 s) is not skippable: it names no current field.
- **Progress while the AI answers** (`content/loop.ts` `progress`, `waitingNow`): the keys still to come are the pass's writes, the fields waiting for the AI (asked this pass, in the books, or arrived and written next pass) and those waiting for their list's options, so a waiting field counts as being filled, never as left for the person; the progress goes again as answers arrive, and "N fields are left for you" shows only once the run rests (until v4, which says it nowhere).
- **Resting at wait-transition** (`FrameReport.resting`; `orchestrator.ts` `resting`; `ui/report/model.ts` `isWorking`): a run that waits for the person's own Next reports `resting: true`, so the panels show the review at rest, never "Autofilling", and the side panel's main button is back.
- **Rows flip on any input** (`orchestrator.ts` `scheduleLeftRefresh`, `refreshLeft`, `currentOf`; `touches.ts` hears trusted `change` and `click` for listeners, never as touches): at rest, `LEFT_REFRESH_MS` (150 ms) after the person's trusted input, click or change anywhere, the fields left for the person are read again, each from its own elements in the scan kept at rest (`readBack`), never a whole scan per keystroke (one fresh scan only when a field's elements left the page): a page's own dropdown's pick, a file chosen through a page's button, a box ticked, and back when it is cleared or unticked (a box holds an answer only while ticked).
- **The side panel at a reload** (`reports.ts` `sideShows`; `frame/hello`'s answer `side`): a top frame that loads while the browser's side panel shows its tab hears it in its hello, so its launcher stays hidden.
- **The launcher** (`background/index.ts`, `background/launcher.ts`, `content/ui/edge.ts`, `page-ui.ts`): `launcher/place` and `launcher/hide` from frame 0 only (`bad_sender` otherwise), `LAUNCHER_LIMIT` (30 a minute per tab), the store's writes one at a time (a keyed queue on `launcher:v1`); the tab's top frame is frame 0, never a frame's own word. A site is kept by its key (`siteKeyOf`): an applicant-tracking host by its platform ("lever"), any other host by the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of its registrable domain (`registrableDomainOf`, without the public suffix list), which a guess could reverse. "Hide until next visit" goes to the worker too, kept in `storage.session` (`launcher-visit:<tabId>`, the top frame's document id) so the side panel offers it back (`panel/launcher` answers `visit`); Show sends `launcher/prefs` with `shown: true`, which undoes it. A held arrow key sends the place once, at its keyup (or the grip's blur). The hide menu opens upward near the window's bottom, a keyboard's opening puts the focus on its first item (ArrowUp, ArrowDown, Home, End move it; Escape and Tab close it), and a trusted press outside the tab closes it. An embedded form's corner launcher hides while the button is hidden on its site or everywhere, except while a run fills (its being seen is S1). After Show, the browser's side panel says the button shows again once it closes (it keeps the tab's launcher hidden meanwhile); the report page says it is back.
- **The lookup on load, narrowed** (`background/job-cards.ts` `loadCard`, `recognisedPosting`, `lookupAddressOf`, `LOOKUP_ON_LOAD_LIMIT`): only for an address a platform's parser reads as an application or a posting (never Workday yet, never the fixture board's generic reading), never in an incognito tab (`Sender.incognito`), never when a card is held or on its way; at most 4 a minute across every tab (a restored session asks a few at a time), never out of the panel's per-frame `JOB_RELAY_LIMIT`; it sends the posting's canonical address, without the page's query string where the posting needs none.
- **The modes a frame hears** (`shared/settings.ts` `ContentModesSchema`; `background/settings.ts` `withShippedModes`): a content script hears only Next and Autofill after page turn; Sign-in and Submit read Manual, stored or saved, until the phase that ships each, whose settings version resets them.
- **Consent boxes on the options page** (`ui/options/Options.tsx`): under All agreements every kind's toggle shows ticked and fixed, with a line pointing to Required only; the kinds pre-approved are kept for it.
- **The cover-letter flag** (`content/runtime.ts` `asksForLetter`): only a file or text box whose label a person can see (`labelVisible`) and which is not camouflaged.
- **Live reports** (`background/reports.ts`): a live report older than the stored one (or as old, over a run's own report) is dropped (`report.stale-live`), so the review never goes back to "Autofilling".
- **A new step under Autofill after page turn: Manually** (`orchestrator.ts` `pageTurned`): the sections and the progress line of the step left behind go too; the review shows no "0 of 0 · 0%" for a step nothing was read on (`Report.tsx` `counted`); Continue is offered, and marked, only while a run waits for it (`Panel.tsx` `awaitsContinue`: a pause, or the page-turned notice; since v4 the main Autofill button sends it, and D7 rests silently at a turned page).
- **The in-page panel's list** (`Report.tsx` `reveal`): the row being written comes into view inside the panel's own scrolling list only, never by scrolling the page around it (the side panel, a page of its own, scrolls as before).
- **A page on a supported host without our script** (`PageState.reload`; `WorkerPlatform.tabs.atsPageOf`): the side panel says "Reload this page to use Autofill" only where the tab's address is one the content script says hello at (a posting, its form, the page after submitting); any other page of an ATS host, where since the v3 Phase 6/7b review's S3 the content script says nothing (a board, a sign-in that names no job), shows "No application form on this page" as before (round 2, K1); the unsupported page lists the sites.
- **The report's look** (`Report.tsx`): every row is folded at first, its icon and label shown (Jobright's list); an optional field left for the person is a grey dash, a required one the alert; the hidden fields left alone are a detail under the lists ("More details") (since v4 an empty circle for either, and hidden or blocked rows left out); the side panel waits for the settings' first answer before it draws the review (no Minimized flash), and the view chosen applies in place. While a run fills, its progress stands where Autofill was (the main button and its strip come back once it rests).

## v3 Phases 3b and 4: the panel's additions, Next and Submit, and "applied" (docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md §12, Decisions 6 to 8)
What follows holds over every note below where they differ. The plan: `wise-percolating-kite.md`, Phase 3 (the part left for 3b) and Phase 4, on TailorIT's autofill API version 3 (`70706b2`).
- **Autofill for another job** (`background/job-cards.ts` `search`, `chooseJob`, `chosenJob`; `ui/panel/AnotherJob.tsx`): `panel/jobSearch {tabId, query}` → GET /api/autofill/jobs/search?q= (`server-client.ts` `searchJobs`: the words in the query string, URL-encoded; the path alone checked against the allow-list), answered as `{ref: 'j0'..'j9', title, company, location}`: TailorIT's ids stay in the worker for the tab's last search. `panel/chooseJob {tabId, ref | null}` keeps the job for the card frame's document (`chosenJobs`, forgotten with the document, the tab and the token); the card's lookup, run/begin's (`run-begin.ts`) and the resume chooser's after a restart send it as `jobId`, so the card, the match, the resume, the cover letter's job and the AI's company and title are that job's. A card read for another choice is not used (`CardEntry.job`). The card says `chosen` (the lookup's `matchedBy: 'chosen'`), with "Use this page's own job".
- **Apply without resume** (`panel/withoutResume {tabId, without}`; `job-cards.ts` `setWithout`, `without`; `planner.ts` `PlannerDeps.withoutResume`; `core/plan/frame.ts` `withoutResumeRow`): the planner attaches no resume (`attachResume(profile, null)`) and planFrame rows a resume upload `resume-not-attached`, skipped, needing the person only where the form requires one. The cover letter and drafts still name the suggested resume as their source. Choosing a resume (setResume) undoes it. The card says `withoutResume`.
- **The resume's preview** (`resume-transfer.ts` `acceptPreview`, the shared `serve`; `ui/panel/preview.ts` `receivePreview`, `ResumePreview.tsx`): the `preview` port, an extension page's only (`index.ts` onConnect), posts `preview/request {tabId}` (`PreviewPortMessageSchema`); the worker resolves the tab's resume (the chosen one, else the card's, read from the list for its kind and name; none while the tab applies without one), fetches and checks it as the resume port does (`prepareResume`: a PDF only, its SHA-256 checked; a Word file is resume-format-unsupported), and sends the header and acknowledged chunks. The panel checks size, chunks and hash, builds a Blob, and shows it from its own `blob:` address in an `iframe` (Chrome's PDF viewer), with "Open it larger"; the address is revoked when the preview closes.
- **Your autofill information** (`background/profile-editor.ts`; `ui/panel/Profile.tsx`, `profile-styles.ts`): `panel/profile` reads GET /profile and, only with `profile:sensitive`, GET /profile/sensitive, for the editor (`ProfileView`: revision, profile, the note's flagged lines, `canWrite` = `profile:write`, the sensitive part or why not). `panel/saveProfile {revision, profile, sensitive?}` checks the save as TailorIT will (`AutofillProfileSaveSchema`, `AutofillSensitiveSaveSchema`: 'invalid' without a request), refuses a sensitive part the connection may not write ('not-allowed'), then PUT /profile; a 409 is 'conflict' (the editor offers Load the latest, the edits kept until then), 400 'invalid', 403 'not-allowed'. A save made, or one TailorIT may have made, drops the worker's profile cache (`ProfileCache.drop`), so the next plan reads it as saved. 20 reads and saves a minute. The editor validates each field with the same schemas and names each problem by its field; read-only without `profile:write`. "Your autofill information" on the card and at the panel's foot opens it in place of the panel's content, with Back (since v4, the set-up view's step 2 and the Settings view's "Autofill information").
- **Edit with AI's instruction** (`content/ui/ai-edits.ts` `openPrompt`; `page-ui.ts`; `orchestrator.ts` `rewrite`; `draft/rewrite`'s `instruction`; `ai-tasks.ts` `draftBatches`): the button opens a box in the closed root under it ("Tell me what to add, change or improve", 750 characters, Enter generates, Shift+Enter a new line, Escape closes); Generate (trusted, on a visible button) sends the field with the instruction; the worker sends it as the rewritten question's `instruction` (AskContext.instruction), withheld as `llm-disabled-for-category` when it names a sensitive topic or states a sensitive answer, a contact detail or a postal address (`namesSensitiveTopic`, `namesSensitiveAnswer`). The box says what came of it (`RewriteOutcome`: the refusal for a sensitive one, the same text, a failure) and closes on a rewrite. The box's keys, the text typed (textInput, beforeinput, input), composition, clipboard, selection and drag and drop events stop at it and at the host in their bubbling phase (the audit of 2026-11-06, S-8); a page's capture listeners can still hear them (docs/PRIVACY.md).
- **The cover letter typed** (docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md §12 Decision 7; `core/plan/frame.ts` `planCoverLetterText`; `core/resolver/resolve.ts`, `values.ts` `freeText.coverLetter`; `core/guard/provenance.ts`; `background/cover-letters.ts` `RunLetter.wait(keys, context, text)`): a cover-letter text box waits for the run's letter as an upload does (`PlanOutput.coverLetter.text`), and the worker reads the ready letter's words (GET …/:id/text, `MAX_LETTER_TEXT` 20,000; never in a dry run), held in the run's memory (`CoverLetterState.text`; `textUnavailable` when they could not be read). planFrame then plans them as a text write (provenance `profile`, path `COVER_LETTER_TEXT_PATH` `coverLetter.text`), through guardWrite and the frame's writer: the canaries, the visibility and label screens and the box's maxLength apply. The Guard lets that path into `freeText.coverLetter` alone, as text, untransformed. Rows: `cover-letter-typed` (the person's), `cover-letter-written` (the AI's, marked "AI"), `prefill-kept` for a box holding text; never an AI-written letter in a profile-only run or with AI-written answers off; a dry run rows a ready letter as the fill would, reading no words.
- **Ask TailorIT to support this site** (`background/site-requests.ts`; `WorkerPlatform.tabs.hostOf`; `Panel.tsx` `AskForSite`): `panel/siteRequest {tabId}` reads the tab's host from Chrome (only http or https; Chrome names it for a tab outside the ATS hosts only under `activeTab`, the toolbar icon's grant for that tab until it navigates) and sends POST /site-requests {host}; without a host, 'no-host' and nothing is sent. Five a minute. The manifest gains `activeTab` (check-build holds the list).
- **The lookup on load for postings** (`frames.ts` `postingAddress`, `cardFrameOf`; `pageOfTab`'s `posting`): the worker reads a top frame's address at its hello (never the frame's word): a posting a platform's parser and TailorIT's parseAtsUrl both read (a Lever or Ashby overview), with no form, is the tab's card frame where it shows no form; its hello sets off the card's lookup as a form's does (same budget, canonical address, never incognito), and its frame relays the card's lookups and Add (`runtime.ts` `job/refresh`). The side panel shows its card with the no-form strip in place of Autofill (`PageState.posting`).
- **The step's buttons** (`content/press.ts` `stepButtonsOf`; `FrameReport.buttons {final, next, submit, pressed?}`; `orchestrator.ts` `buttonsNow`): at rest (review, or waiting for the person's Next) and never in a dry run, each report says the adapter's finality and whether its Next and Submit show (visible, enabled). Greenhouse, Lever and Ashby are one step: 'final' with Submit; the wizard's test adapter reports 'not-final' with Next.
- **Next and Submit pressed for the person** (`content/press.ts` `pressStep`; `dom/fillers/not-submit.ts` `clickAdapterSubmit`; `messaging` `panel/press`, `run/press`, `PressAnswer`; `background/panel.ts` `press`; `ui/report/StepDock.tsx`; `content/ui/panel.tsx`): the dock shows "Continue to the next page" or "Submit application" for the frame whose report offers it. The side panel's click sends `panel/press {tabId, frameId, target, confirmed}`; the worker relays it only while that frame's report offers the button, as `run/press` (taken by the runtime only from the worker); the in-page panel presses its own frame's run on a trusted click it saw. `FillRun.press` answers 'busy' while the run works, reads back what it wrote (E9), then `pressStep`: 'blocked' while a pause (sign-in, code, CAPTCHA) shows, or for Submit any unsolved CAPTCHA; 'not-offered' unless the adapter's finality fits (Next: 'not-final'; Submit: 'final') and its button shows; for Submit, 'required-empty' with the count of required fields a person can see still empty (one per question), unless `confirmed`; then the one click, `clickIfAllowed` with `allowNext`, or `clickAdapterSubmit` ('refused' when the check says no). `clickAdapterSubmit` (the Submit exception) presses exactly the adapter's `submitButton` element on a final step, never a Next, never a never-pressed button, nothing inside or around it; lint and `test/allow-submit.test.ts` hold it to `press.ts` (the review, below).
- **Submit mode Auto** (`core/steps/machine.ts` `autoSubmitVerdict`, `SUBMIT_REFUSALS`, the `press-submit` effect, `RunContext.autoSubmit`, `StepFacts.submitVisible`, `requiredEmpty`, `noAi`, `legalGuess`; `orchestrator.ts` `submitFacts`, `autoSubmit`; settings `modes.submit` shown, `ContentModesSchema` with `submit` and `agentSkip`; `background/settings.ts` `withShippedModes` now forces Sign-in alone): a final step validated clean goes to review, then press-submit when the verdict passes (the mode on, final, Submit shown, no error, filled, the skip rules, no unconfirmed draft, no unsolved CAPTCHA, settled); else review with `submit-held-required` (its count), `-no-ai`, `-guess`, `-captcha` or `-check`. `noAi` is the posting's own word (policy, policy-profile-only, policy-stored-copy notices), never an "I did not use AI" statement a profile-only fill makes true; `legalGuess` an AI guess on a legal category. Inside another page the press waits for the frame to be seen (S1).
- **Submission detection and "applied"** (`content/runtime.ts` `arm`, `detected`, `personSubmitted`; `content/submitted.ts` `successShown`, `SUBMIT_CONFIRM_MS`; `adapters/greenhouse/url.ts` reads `/<board>/jobs/<id>/confirmation` as the confirmation page; `background/submissions.ts`; `PageState.submitted`): a Submit is armed by our press (`RunEnv.submitPressed`), the person's trusted primary click on the adapter's Submit, or a trusted submit of the form within 1 s of their Enter in it (the submit event alone is trusted whoever clicked, a page's own `click()` included, so it never counts by itself). `submit/armed {applicationKey, by, runId?}` is answered before any sign is sent; the worker checks the key against Chrome's address and keeps the arm per tab and application for `SUBMIT_ARM_MS` (10 minutes), with the posting's canonical address (`lookupAddressOf`), the run's job id (the chosen job's, else the lookup's) and attached resume. The document then watches (a MutationObserver, until `SUBMIT_CONFIRM_MS`, 45 s) for the platform's sign: Ashby's `.ashby-application-form-success-container`, Greenhouse's `.confirmation .confirmation__content`, each visible; an address change to a confirmation page; a new document opening on one (Lever's /thanks, Greenhouse's /confirmation). `submit/detected {applicationKey, evidence}` is taken only for an arm of the same tab and application, fresh, once; then POST /applied ({jobId} else {url}, the resume), its lookup kept as the frame's card, and the tab's `submitted` state ('recording', then 'recorded', 'not-recorded' or 'not-allowed') pushed to the side panel ("Application submitted", "Open tracker" to TailorIT's /tracker; the card read again, so it shows Applied). The run, told by the runtime (`FillRun.submittedSeen`, or the stored RunState on a new document), takes SUBMITTED: done. A press of ours no sign followed within 45 s raises `submit-unconfirmed` and offers Submit again. The state goes with the document that saw it, the tab, or the token (the arms).

### The v3 Phase 4 review (2026-11-02)
What follows holds over the bullets above where they differ.
- **Required questions left** (`press.ts` `requiredLeftOf`): a hidden native control a visible one stands for counts (a file input behind its upload label: `hidden-file-with-visible-control`; a radio or box behind its label), and so does each of the run's own required rows still left for the person (`leftForPerson`: a resume a fill attached none to under "Apply without resume", a failed upload, a widget the scan cannot read), unless its field reads answered now. The panel's Submit asks first, and Submit mode Auto holds (`submit-held-required`), on that count.
- **The page's refusal**: `pressStep` listens for the form's own `invalid` events during its one click: a form whose validation refused it answers `not-accepted` with how many fields it marked. `refusalAfter` (PRESS_REFUSAL_MS, 2 s after a press) reads the adapter's visible errors, the fields marked invalid meanwhile and Ashby's `.ashby-application-form-failure-container`: a Submit's wait ends at once (`FillRun.submitRefused`, notice `submit-errors` with the count), and a panel Next that left the step where it was with errors shown answers `not-accepted`. The dock says so with the count.
- **The Submit exception, structurally confined** (`not-submit.ts` `clickAdapterSubmit`): `NotSubmitContext` has no Submit flag; `clickAdapterSubmit(adapter, root)` finds the adapter's Submit itself and checks it with an unexported symbol key, so `assertNotSubmit` and `clickIfAllowed` refuse Submit for every caller. It is not exported from `dom/fillers/index.ts`, and `eslint.config.mjs` (`SUBMIT_PRESS_RULE`) refuses its name (an import, a re-export, a property read) everywhere but `src/content/press.ts`; `test/allow-submit.test.ts` holds both. `run/press` is taken only from the worker's own script address (`runtime.ts` `fromWorker`: the sender's URL is the extension's `/background.js`), never from an extension page.
- **Submit mode Auto** (`orchestrator.ts` `autoSubmit`): after its last wait it reads again that the mode is on and the run is no dry run, the step is the one validated (`movedOn`), nothing the run wrote changed since (`lookBeforeLeaving`), and the page shows no error; it presses with `confirmed: !skipRules.requiredEmpty` (so the press counts the required questions again while that rule holds); once per step per document (`RunEnv.autoPressed`, whichever run). `policy-hidden-text` holds it (`submit-held-no-ai`: a statement all the same); `policy-unreadable` does not, and the review then says it was submitted anyway (`submit-auto-unchecked`); the tripwire's hits never hold it. A confirmation that comes while the run still works is kept (`submittedLate`) and taken as SUBMITTED as soon as the run rests.
- **Detection** (`runtime.ts` `arm`, `submitted.ts` `newSuccessSign`): the arm records the application key and platform at the press (so a sign drawn after the page moved to an address no parser reads still counts, for that application), the form root and adapter, and the platform's success signs already on the page just before the press (`RunEnv.beforePress`, a `PressSnapshot` read before the click, since the click may move the page before the press is armed; the person's own Submit at their click, before the page's handlers). A sign counts only when it is new, shown, the form root gone or hidden, and outside the posting's own text (`Adapter.postingText`). A person's own Submit carries the id of the run that filled the form (`submit/armed` `runId`), and `resume` when that run's resume row verified. A watch that ends without a confirmation clears the run's wait (`submitWatchEnded`; `submitUnconfirmed` for a press of ours), a page arm included; a newer press replaces it silently.
- **Recording** (`background/submissions.ts`): the arm (ids, keys and times: when, by whom, frame, document, job id, resume id, when its sign was taken) is kept in `storage.session` (`submit-arm:`), the posting's address in memory only; the tab's page state too (`submitted:`), so a worker Chrome stopped while the person solved a check still records it and the panel still shows it. A confirmation page counts only from the frame that pressed and within CONFIRMATION_PAGE_MS (60 s) of the press; a success message within SUBMIT_ARM_MS. Never in a private tab; never before the disclosure as it reads now is acknowledged; a person's own Submit with no run of ours only while `modes.recordOwn` is on ("Record applications I submit myself", shown in Modes, on by default; a stored version 4 value without it reads as on). The job is the one chosen for the application now (`chosenJob(tabId, applicationKey)`), else the run's lookup's; the resume only with the frame's `resume`. The arm goes once TailorIT recorded it or refused it for good (400, 403, 404, 401); offline or a server failure leaves `not-recorded` and the panel's Try again (`panel/retryApplied`). The page state goes with the document that saw it, when the frame moves to another posting (frame/hello's `nav` with another application key: `submissions.moved`), or with the tab.
- **The chosen job and "Apply without resume"** (`job-cards.ts`): kept per tab for the card frame's application (`FrameEntry.applicationKey`, the worker's own reading of the frame's address at its hello; the same document at an address no parser reads keeps the key it had, so an arm the press itself moved there still fits), so a Lever posting and its /apply page, or Ashby's Overview and Application, keep them; another application lets them go (`applicationShown`, from frame/hello for the card frame), and so does a token change. A search's ids go with the page it was made on, and only its own page's search chooses. The resume chooser follows a new choice mid-run (`resume-choice.ts` `followJob`: the lookup asked again with the job chosen now, its posting text with it), so the run's job facts, its resume and the job a submission records are the tab's choice now. A failed choice says so in the panel.
- **Confirmation pages** (`frames.ts` `confirmationAddress`; `PageState.confirmation`; a frame's card is kept across its document's own address moves when the application stays the same and Chrome's address is the tab's): a top frame with no form on the platform's page after submitting is the card frame where there is no form, but only for the card POST /applied answered (never looked up through). The side panel shows "Application submitted", naming the job, with Open Job Tracker (and Try again when not recorded), the card with Applied, no Autofill; the dock reads "Submitted" (sticky at the panel's bottom), and the review list offers no Cancel or Skip.
- **The resume's preview**: one transfer per preview opened (the panel's loader is stable per tab; the preview remounts for another resume), and closing it disconnects a transfer still on its way.
- **The in-page dock** (`content/ui/panel.tsx`): the dock's own button is watched (IntersectionObserver v2, `visible.ts` `shownFor`), and its click counts only once it has been seen for `CONTROL_SEEN_MS` (600 ms since the audit of 2026-11-06, as every control of ours: below; 500 ms before).
- **The disclosure's version** (`shared/stored.ts` `DISCLOSURE_VERSION` 2, `DISCLOSURE_KEY`; `auth.ts` `disclosed`, `acknowledgeDisclosure`; `AuthStatus.disclosure`; `ui/page/disclosure.tsx`): kept in `storage.local` at pairing and at I understand; an install connected before it reads version 1. Until the current version is acknowledged: Submit stays Manual in what frames are told (`contentSettingsOf` `holdSubmit`, at hello, run/begin and every push), no arm is taken, and no page is looked up as it loads.
- **The profile editor** (the review's 16): Load the latest keeps the person's edits on the latest (each value, list or sensitive answer they changed; one changed elsewhere too keeps theirs and is named); a 409 drops the worker's profile cache too; an email equal to the account's sign-in address is shown empty and saved as '' (TailorIT's "follow the account"); countries from the ISO 3166-1 list, entry dates as month pickers; a salary of 0 asks for an amount above 0.
- **The cover letter typed** (17, 18): a letter longer than the box's maxLength is `cover-letter-too-long` (never cut), its row saying the letter's length and the box's limit (`detail.letterLength`, counts only); the canaries RunState keeps are checked against the profile without the letter's words; a read of the words answered 409 or failed on the way is made once more about 2 s later, only while the letter is still ready.

## v3 Phase 5: the auto-apply agent and Sign-in Auto (docs/SPEC-AMENDMENTS.md §12, Decisions 9 and 10)
What follows holds over every note below where they differ. The plan: `wise-percolating-kite.md`, Phase 5, on TailorIT's autofill API version 3 (its routes: `GET /api/autofill/agent/next`, `POST /api/autofill/agent/:id/result`, `GET /api/autofill/credentials?host=`; TailorIT's `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Jobright parity, TailorIT's side").
- **The agent** (`background/agent.ts` `createAgent`, `agentVerdict`, `agentAddressOf`): a state machine in the worker, its operations one at a time (a keyed queue). Options › Auto-apply › "Run auto-apply" (`agent/set {on, confirmed?}`) turns it on only while the connection holds `agent:run` and the disclosure was acknowledged, and only with the person's one-time confirmation (kept with the switch, `storage.local` `agent`). `tokenChanged` (a connection made, changed or dropped) sets the switch off and the confirmation unasked, and ends a running job (needs-you `cancelled`, its tab released and closed, its result kept for a connection to tell). While on, or while a result waits to be told, the `agent-tick` alarm (`chrome.alarms`, every 30 s: the `alarms` permission) and the worker's own timers call `tick()`:
  - the record of another browser session is the person's: `agent:run` carries `session`, the nonce of the browser session its window and tab ids belong to (`storage.session` `agent:session`, 16 random bytes made on first need, gone at a browser restart); a running record with another nonce is finished needs-you `cancelled` with its ids nulled, nothing released or closed (`adopt`), and no message of a tab with its old id is the agent's (`activeRun` needs the nonce);
  - a result not yet told is sent again (POST …/result) while the connection holds `agent:run`: taken, or refused for good, it is dropped; a `submitted` result is kept for every refusal but 404, 400 and a path refused before any request, any other only for TailorIT's failure or none (offline, a timeout, 5xx, 429, a version mismatch); the status's `pending` says one waits;
  - off, or blocked (not connected, no `agent:run`, the disclosure not current), a running job is finished needs-you `cancelled` and its tab released and closed;
  - the running job (`storage.local` `agent:run`: the queue id, the task's application key, job and resume ids, the job's company and title, the window and tab ids with their session's nonce, times, the runs started and the documents a run was started or an entry pressed in, when and in which document its Submit was pressed, when a form was held after it, the retry, the AI guesses per step, the result; never an address) is looked at: its tab gone (the person closed it) is needs-you `cancelled`, nothing closed, never opened again; past `AGENT_JOB_MAX_MS` (25 min) needs-you `timed_out`; no form within `AGENT_FORM_WAIT_MS` (60 s) of opening failed `posting_closed` only when the tab's address is no posting's (`atsPageOf(tab).page === 'other'`), or a frame of it said hello before the job began (after it: needs-you `other`), and `page_error` while the tab is still at the job's address or its address is unknown (the audit of 2026-11-06, M5); a form held after the job's Submit (below) unconfirmed `AGENT_CONFIRM_MS` (90 s) later needs-you `other` (failed `validation_error` after the retry); then its reports, then `AGENT_JOB_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 min) without progress needs-you `timed_out` (progress: a report not read before, or a run at work, its AI answers awaited, which a window Chrome throttles in the background stretches; never `done` or a Submit's confirmation awaited);
  - otherwise, past the backoff (`storage.session` `agent:poll`: a minute after an empty queue or a failure, then five) and while a normal window of the person's is open (`windows.othersOpen`), GET …/agent/next: `{item: null}` waits; a task with no canonical application address the agent fills (`agentAddressOf`: `agentApplicationUrl`, on an ATS host, with the adapters' application key) is failed `unsupported_site`, never opened; else the record is stored (with that key), then a window opened (`windows.open`: `chrome.windows.create({url: 'about:blank', focused: false, type: 'normal'})`) and the canonical address sent to its tab (`windows.navigate`: `tabs.update`), so the job's tab is known before anything of it loads. A record whose window was never opened (a worker stopped between) is opened from the row TailorIT gives back to this connection, when its address's key is the record's; a record TailorIT no longer runs is dropped.
  - **Its run:** `frame/hello` from the agent's tab's top frame is the task's only with its application key: none (the platform's board, its "not found") is failed `posting_closed`, another posting's needs-you `other`. Each names the task's job and resume as the tab's chosen ones (`deps.assign`, `job-cards.ts` `assign`), so run/begin's lookup and the resume attached are the task's. With a form: `run/start {agent: true}` to that document once, at most `AGENT_MAX_STARTS` (8) a job (then needs-you `other`), and never once the job's Submit was pressed (the form is held: `heldAt`). Without a form (and not the platform's page after submitting) `agent/enter`, once per document and at most `AGENT_MAX_ENTRIES` (2) a job: 'blocked' maps its `block` (`sign-in` → `sign_in`, `sign-in-failed`, `email-code` → `email_code`, `captcha`, `other`), 'not-offered' waits (a form drawn late). The content script's `run/start {agent}` (`runtime.ts` `startAgentRun`) resumes the tab's stored RunState for the application (Continue's way: its step attempts, the runaway-wizard count and its carried rows go on across documents), a fresh run only without one (or a stopped one), never a done one; a run the page's load resumed by itself is waited for (`AGENT_RESUMED_WAIT_MS`, 30 s) and continued. The worker answers that tab's `frame/hello`, `run/begin` (any trigger) and `release`'s `settings/changed` for the task's application, while the agent may run (`agent.task(tabId, key, documentId)`), with `contentSettingsOf(..., {agent: true, agentSubmitHeld})`: Next, Submit and Autofill after page turn on Auto (Submit Manual in any document but the one the job's Submit was pressed in, once it was), "Ask me each time" at a no-AI posting as Profile only, the person's skip rules and Sign-in mode. `run/begin {trigger: 'agent'}` from any other tab, or for another application, is `bad_message`.
  - **Its watch:** every `report/put` and `report/live` of the agent's tab (`agent.reported`) reads the top frame's report through `agentVerdict`: working, a Submit pressed (`buttons.pressed`, which also records the press), `done` and a dry run wait; the policy card is skipped `posting_forbids_ai`; a sign-in or account page is needs-you `sign_in` (Sign-in Auto signing in: `sign-in-auto` waits `AGENT_SIGN_IN_WAIT_MS`, 60 s; `sign-in-failed` is `sign_in_failed`); a pause is needs-you `captcha` (CAPTCHA, bot check), `email_code`, failed `page_error` (still busy) or needs-you `other`; stopped is failed `page_error` (form hidden) or needs-you `cancelled`; at rest, the skip rules' notices (`submit-held-no-ai` skipped `posting_forbids_ai`, `submit-held-required` needs-you `required_field_empty`, `submit-held-guess` needs-you `ai_guess_legal`), a CAPTCHA (`captcha`), a sign-in (`sign_in`), the page refusing the Submit pressed (`submit-errors`: one retry, `run/start {agent, retry}`, which lets Submit mode Auto press the step once more in the same document, the retried run's reports no longer counting; a retry the frame refuses, and a second refusal, failed `validation_error`), errors before any Submit (`validation`: failed `validation_error`, the run's own fix pass came first), `submit-unconfirmed` (needs-you `other`), `final-unknown` (needs-you `unsupported_site`), a step at its limit or a runaway wizard (failed `validation_error`), a form out of view (failed `page_error`), a Submit held for another check (`submit-held-check`: needs-you `other`, never retried: nothing was pressed); nothing decisive for `AGENT_SETTLE_MS` (15 s) is needs-you `other`. AI guesses are the most rows with reason `ai-guess` a report of each step showed.
  - **Submitted:** the Submit Submit mode Auto pressed is armed as any (`submit/armed {by: 'auto'}`), which records the job's press and its document (`agent.pressed`); its platform's sign (`submit/detected`, taken by `submissions.ts` only for an arm of the same tab) goes to `agent.submitted(tabId, applicationKey)` for the agent's tab instead of POST /applied: for the task's application, POST …/agent/:id/result `{state: 'submitted', reason: null, aiGuesses}`, which TailorIT takes as Applied, the tab's page state following ('recorded' or 'not-recorded'); for another posting's, the job is needs-you `other` and the sign is recorded the normal way (POST /applied).
  - **Finish:** the result is stored, the agent's tab released (`deps.release`: `run/cancel` and the person's own settings to each frame of ours there) and that tab closed (`windows.closeTab`: `tabs.remove`; only a tab of this browser session that is still there, never a window), the result sent (above); the tally (`storage.local` `agent:day`: the day, the count, the last result) counts it; the next job is taken a second later.
  - **Stop:** `agent/stop` and `agent/set {on: false}` leave the job running for the person (needs-you `cancelled`) and stop the alarm unless a result waits to be told; the person closing its tab or window (`tabs.onRemoved`, or a look that finds the tab gone) does too, closing nothing, and since the audit of 2026-11-06 (m14) it also pauses the agent (`pause('window-closed')`: Run auto-apply off, `confirmed` kept, the reason in `AgentStatus.paused`), so no next job opens until the person turns it on again.
  - **Outages** (the audit of 2026-11-06, M5): no job is taken while `navigator.onLine` is false (`AGENT_BLOCKS` `offline`, cleared by itself); the agent pauses itself (`AGENT_PAUSES`: `no-window`, `page-errors`, `window-closed`) when `windows.open()` gives no window (that job needs-you `cancelled`) and after `AGENT_MAX_PAGE_ERRORS` (3) `page_error` results in a row (`storage.session` `agent:errors`; any other result resets it). The person's own off or Stop clears the reason; turning it on clears it and the count.
  - **Status** (`agent/status`, value-free `AgentStatus`: on, confirmed, phase off/waiting/running/blocked, why blocked, whether it may be turned on, whether a result waits to be told, the job's company and title, the jobs done today, the last state and reason): the options page's Auto-apply card and the side panel's strip (`ui/page/agent.tsx`; the strip shows while on or while a result waits: "· a result not yet told TailorIT"), read again on `panel/changed {what: 'agent'}`, which the worker posts whenever it changes. A link opens TailorIT's `/auto-apply`.
  - **A background window still fills:** the agent's form is its window's top frame, where the run's S1 visibility check never applies; the fill's waits are timers and MutationObservers (`dom/time.ts`, `dom/settle.ts`, `content/transition.ts`), none of them `requestAnimationFrame` (only the launcher's layout uses it) or IntersectionObserver (only a subframe's S1 check and the in-page controls' trusted-click gates, which the agent never needs: its presses go through `press.ts` and `not-submit.ts`). An occluded window's timers may be throttled to once a second, and after about five minutes hidden Chrome's intensive throttling batches them once a minute, which can stretch the waits for the AI's answers: a run at work counts as progress (above), so only `AGENT_JOB_MAX_MS` bounds it.
- **The agent's entry** (`adapters/entry.ts` `applicationLinkOf`, `Adapter.entryControl`, `Adapter.entryNeverPressed`; `not-submit.ts` `clickAdapterEntry`; `content/press.ts` `pressEntry`; `runtime.ts` `enterForAgent`): `agent/enter`, from the worker's own script alone (`runtime.ts` `fromWorker`), in the top frame with no form: the platform adapter's declared control, pressed by `clickAdapterEntry`, which finds it itself and refuses one that submits a form, one a person cannot see or press, any while the adapter finds an application form, and one the adapter's `neverPress` (read over the whole page) lists, holds or sits in, unless the adapter marks its entry as that one (`entryNeverPressed`: Ashby's Application tab, that element alone); `pressEntry` answers 'blocked' with what the pause shows (`EntryAnswer.block`: a sign-in or account page, an email code, a CAPTCHA, another) and watches a page that draws it late for `ENTRY_WAIT_MS` (8 s); a sign-in page blocking it is signed in to once with Sign-in mode Auto (`enterForAgent`: then 'pressed', or `sign-in-failed`). Greenhouse's is the job post header's `button[type=button][aria-label="Apply"]` (its snapshots), Lever's and Ashby's the link (Ashby: `a[role=tab]`) whose address the platform's own parser reads as this posting's application form (Lever's posting page was never captured, so no class name is used). Lint (`ENTRY_PRESS_RULE`) holds its name to `press.ts`, and `ENTRY_WRAPPER_RULE` and `SIGN_IN_WRAPPER_RULE` hold `pressEntry` and `signIn` to `runtime.ts` (with `test/allow-submit.test.ts`, which also proves `not-submit.ts` never calls a door itself).
- **Sign-in Auto** (`dom/sign-in-form.ts`, `content/sign-in.ts`, `background/sign-in.ts`, `orchestrator.ts` `paused`/`signInNow`, `not-submit.ts` `clickAdapterSignIn`):
  - `modes.signIn` reaches the content script (`ContentModesSchema`) as it acts: Manual unless the token holds `credentials:read` and the disclosure was acknowledged (`index.ts` `holds`). The options page and the gear offer Auto only then (`ui/page/modes.tsx` `signInAvailability`), saying how otherwise.
